is singular, therefore
\lambda_{\min}(\Theta(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)}))=0
.
∎

Lemma G.4
.

For any
x^{(1:N)}
such that no
x^{(i)}
is zero vector, we have at least
2N-1
linearly independent vectors that

\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})v=0,

so that
\mathrm{rank}\ \Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\leq N(d_{0}+1)-N
.

Proof.

Let’s first define the following vectors:

v_{i}=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{(i-1)\cdot(d_{0}+1)},-2,x_{0}^{(i)},\ldots,x_{d_{0}-1}^{(i)},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{(N-i)\cdot(d_{0}+1)})^{\intercal}

for
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Then these vectors are linearly independent, and for
i\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})v_{j}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)}=

\displaystyle-4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle^{2}-2\left\|x^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left\|x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}+2\left\|x^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left(x^{(j)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)},

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})v_{j}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)+\alpha}

\displaystyle=-8\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle x_{\alpha}^{(j)}-4\left\|x^{(j)}\right\|^{2}x_{\alpha}^{(i)}

\displaystyle+4x_{\alpha}^{(i)}\left(x^{(j)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}+4x_{\alpha}^{(j)}\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}+4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle x_{\alpha}^{(j)},

showing that they all lies in the null space of
\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})
.

We then define the following vectors:

w_{i}=(0,-x_{0}^{(i)},\ldots,-x_{d_{0}-1}^{(i)}\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{(i-2)\cdot(d_{0}+1)},0,x_{0}^{(1)},\ldots,x_{d_{0}-1}^{(1)},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{(N-i)\cdot(d_{0}+1)})^{\intercal}

for
i=2,\ldots,N
.

Similarly for
i\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})w_{j}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)}=

\displaystyle-4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(1)}\rangle\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}-2\left\|x^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left(x^{(1)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}

\displaystyle+4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(1)}+2\left\|x^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left(x^{(j)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(1)},

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})w_{j}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)+\alpha}=

\displaystyle-4x_{\alpha}^{(i)}\left(x^{(1)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}-4x_{\alpha}^{(1)}\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(j)}-4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(1)}\rangle x_{\alpha}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+4x_{\alpha}^{(i)}\left(x^{(j)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(1)}+4x_{\alpha}^{(j)}\left(x^{(i)}\right)^{\intercal}x^{(1)}+4\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle x_{\alpha}^{(1)}

showing that these vectors also lie in the null space of
\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})
.

It is clear that all vectors
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}\cup\{w_{j}\}_{j=2}^{N}
are linearly independent.
These show that the null space is at least
N+N-1
dimension, therefore the rank of

\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\leq N(d_{0}+1)-2N+1.

∎

Lemma G.5
.

For any
x^{(1:N)}
, we have

\mathrm{rank}\ \dot{\Sigma}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\leq d_{0}.

Proof.

Instead of giving the eigendecomposition, we show that there exists
d_{0}
vectors that

\dot{\Sigma}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})=\sum_{\alpha=0}^{d_{0}-1}\lambda_{\alpha}v_{\alpha}v_{\alpha}^{\intercal}

for
\lambda_{\alpha}\geq 0
and
v_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{N(d_{0}+1)}
.

We define these vectors as

\displaystyle v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=(x_{\alpha}^{(1)},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{\alpha},1,\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d_{0}-\alpha-1},x_{\alpha}^{(2)},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{\alpha},1,\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d_{0}-\alpha-1},\ldots,x_{\alpha}^{(N)},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{\alpha},1,\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d_{0}-\alpha-1})^{\intercal}

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left(v_{\gamma}v_{\gamma}^{\intercal}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1),j(d_{0}+1)}

\displaystyle=x_{\gamma}^{(i)}x_{\gamma}^{(j)},

\displaystyle\left(v_{\gamma}v_{\gamma}^{\intercal}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)+\alpha,j(d_{0}+1)}

\displaystyle=x_{\gamma}^{(j)}\mathbb{I}_{\alpha=\gamma},

\displaystyle\left(v_{\gamma}v_{\gamma}^{\intercal}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1),j(d_{0}+1)+\beta}

\displaystyle=x_{\gamma}^{(i)}\mathbb{I}_{\beta=\gamma},

\displaystyle\left(v_{\gamma}v_{\gamma}^{\intercal}\right)_{i(d_{0}+1)+\alpha,j(d_{0}+1)+\beta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{\alpha=\gamma}\mathbb{I}_{\beta=\gamma}

which shows that taking
\lambda_{\gamma}=4
proves the identity.
∎

Remark G.6
.

We note that the bound of Lemma
G.4
is not tight.
If we computationally test this rank condition, the rank of
\Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})
follows

\mathrm{rank}\ \Sigma(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\max(d_{0}-i,0)=\begin{cases}\frac{N(2d_{0}-N+1)}{2}&\text{if }d_{0}>N\\
\frac{d_{0}(d_{0}+1)}{2}&\text{if }d_{0}\leq N\end{cases}

for non-parallel inputs
x^{(1:N)}
.

Conversely, the bound of Lemma
G.5
is tight, i.e.,

\mathrm{rank}\ \dot{\Sigma}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})=d_{0}

for non-parallel inputs
x^{(1:N)}
.

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Before giving the proof, we first state the complete form of
\Theta
in Theorem
4.3
, which is the
1/\kappa^{2}
-scaled version of the limiting JNTK:

\displaystyle\Theta(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\>\prod_{u=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\right),

\displaystyle\Theta(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\Bigg(\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}\>\prod_{u=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\>\sum_{u_{\alpha}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\Theta(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\Bigg(\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}\>\prod_{u=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\>\sum_{u_{\beta}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\beta})}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\Theta(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\Bigg(\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}\>\prod_{u=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}\>\sum_{u_{\beta}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\beta})}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}\>\sum_{u_{\alpha}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\>\sum_{u_{\alpha\beta}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha\beta})}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle+\Sigma^{(l)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{\alpha},u_{\beta}=l\\
u_{\alpha}\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}\>\Gamma^{(u_{\beta})}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}\>\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\\
u\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}\Bigg),

where
\Gamma^{(u)}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}\to\mathbb{R}^{(1+d_{0})\times(1+d_{0})}
is defined as follows: for
g^{(u)}\sim\mathcal{GP}(0,\Sigma^{(u)})
,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{g^{(u)}}\left[\dot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x)_{0})\dot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{0})\right],

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{g^{(u)}}\left[\ddot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x)_{0})\dot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{0})g^{(u)}(x)_{\alpha}\right],

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{g^{(u)}}\left[\dot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x)_{0})\ddot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{0})g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{\beta}\right],

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(u)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{g^{(u)}}\left[\ddot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x)_{0})\ddot{\phi}(g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{0})g^{(u)}(x)_{\alpha}g^{(u)}(x^{\prime})_{\beta}\right].

We prove Theorem
4.3
by using the proof strategy for Theorem
3.1
in Appendix
E
again. That is, we express the computation of backpropagated gradient as a tensor program, and apply Theorem
D.1
to show the claimed convergence in Theorem
4.3
.

Recall the definition of the finite JNTK: for
\alpha,\beta\in[d_{0}]
and
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial\theta}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial\theta}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial\theta}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial\theta}\right\rangle.

We rephrase this finite JNTK such that the contribution of each layer is explicit:

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial W^{(l)}},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial W^{(l)}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial W^{(l)}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial W^{(l)}},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}}\right\rangle.

(11)

By the definitions of the MLP
f
and its components
g^{(l)}
and
h^{(l)}
in Appendix
C
,
for every
l=2,\ldots,L+1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial W^{(l)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)}\left(h^{(l-1)}(x)\right)^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)}\left(h^{(l-1)}(x)\right)^{\intercal}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x)/\partial x_{\alpha-1})}\left(\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)^{\intercal}.

The derivatives with respect to
W^{(1)}
have similar forms shown below:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial W^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(1)}(x)}\left(x\right)^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)}{\partial W^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(1)}(x)}\left(x\right)^{\intercal}+\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(1)}(x)/\partial x_{\alpha-1})}\left(e_{\alpha-1}\right)^{\intercal}.

We plug in these characterisations of the derivatives in the layer-wise description of the finite JNTK in (
11
),
and simplify the results using
\langle v_{1}w_{1}^{\intercal},v_{2}w_{2}^{\intercal}\rangle=\langle v_{1},v_{2}\rangle\cdot\langle w_{1},w_{2}\rangle
. Recall that
C_{l}=d
if
l=2,\ldots,L+1
and
C_{1}=1
.

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

(12)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

(13)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}},h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x)/\partial x_{\alpha-1})},\frac{\partial f(x^{\prime})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

(14)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})}{\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime})}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

(15)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}},h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x)/\partial x_{\alpha-1})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle h^{(l-1)}(x),\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})}{\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x)},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{L+1}\frac{1}{C_{l-1}}\left\langle\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}},\frac{\partial h^{(l-1)}(x^{\prime})}{\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x)/\partial x_{\alpha-1})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{\prime})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{\prime})}\right\rangle.

(16)

So it is enough to show that all the inner products here converge to constants. Then, we have the convergence of the finite JNTK at initialisation. The tensor program in Appendix
E
already computes both components
of the first inner product of each summand. Applying the Master theorem to these inner products normalised by
C_{l-1}
gives the almost-sure convergence of these normalised inner products to
\Sigma^{(l-1)}(x,x^{\prime})_{00},\Sigma^{(l-1)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0},\Sigma^{(l-1)}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta},\Sigma^{(l-1)}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}
. Now it remains to show the convergence of the second inner product of each summand. We do this by expressing the backpropagated gradients in a tensor program.

We first include a new random vector for the initialisation,
V^{(L+1)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which is independent of all other vectors, and its entries are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\kappa^{2})
.
The body of this program first includes the variables that appeared in Section
E
, and additionally the following variables:

\displaystyle Dh_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,Dh_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,Dh_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,Dh_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle Dg_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,Dg_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,Dg_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,Dg_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(1)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)},\ldots,D_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

These variables are defined as follows:

\displaystyle Dh_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq V^{(L+1)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle Dh_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq V^{(L+1)},

\displaystyle Dg_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(L)}\right)\odot Dh_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle Dg_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(L)}\right)\odot Dh_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle Dh_{1}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}Dg_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle Dh_{N}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}Dg_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle Dg_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(1)}\right)\odot Dh_{1}^{(1)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle Dg_{N}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(1)}\right)\odot Dh_{N}^{(1)}.

These random vectors correspond to the backpropagated gradient, but we need the scaling as

\displaystyle Dh_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial f(x^{(i)})}{\partial h^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},

\displaystyle Dg_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial f(x^{(i)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}

where this
\sqrt{d}
scaling comes from the scaling at output
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(V^{(L+1)}\right)^{\intercal}h_{i}^{(L)}
.

Similarly, we define the backpropagation of the Jacobian gradient, defined as

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq V^{(L+1)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq V^{(L+1)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(L)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(L)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(1)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(1)},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(1)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(1)},

and

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbf{0},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbf{0},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(L)}\right)\odot J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)}\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)}+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(L)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(L)}\right)\odot J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)}\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)}+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(L)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}D_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(L-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(V^{(L)}\right)^{\intercal}D_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(L)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(1)}\right)\odot J_{\alpha}g_{1}^{(1)}\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(1)}+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{1}^{(1)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}h_{1}^{(1)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(1)}\right)\odot J_{\alpha}g_{N}^{(1)}\odot D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(1)}+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{N}^{(1)}\right)\odot D_{\alpha}h_{N}^{(1)}.

Again, these random vectors correspond to the backpropagated gradient with scaling, as

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\left(\frac{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\left(\frac{\partial h^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right)},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}.

To apply the Master theorem, we need to check that (1) the nonlinear functions we applied are polynomially bounded and (2) the program satisfies the BP-likeness property.
We have three non-linear functions to check for polynomial boundedness:
\phi
,
\dot{\phi}(a)\cdot b
, and
\ddot{\phi}(a)\cdot b\cdot c+\dot{\phi}(a)+d
.
All of these functions are polynomially bounded, since they are linear combinations of products of
\phi,\dot{\phi},\ddot{\phi}
to the linear functions, where
\phi,\dot{\phi},\ddot{\phi}
is Lipschitz by assumption.
For the BP-likeness, the simple GIA check (Condition 1 of
(
Yang, 2020
)
) again applies to our program, since
V^{(L+1)}
is not used in any other part of the program, except when we compute the output.

The outputs of the program whose convergence is guaranteed by Theorem
D.1
are followings: for
i,j\in[N]
,
\alpha,\beta\in[d_{0}]
and
l\in[N]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x^{(i)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial f(x^{(j)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(17)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial f(x^{(j)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(18)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})/\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)})},\frac{\partial f(x^{(j)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(19)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(20)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x^{(i)})}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(21)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(22)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})/\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(23)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(24)

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(i)})/\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)})},\frac{\partial J(f)(x^{(j)})_{\beta-1}}{\partial(\partial g^{(l)}(x^{(j)})/\partial x_{\beta-1}^{(j)})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right].

(25)

Our final computation is evaluating these nine expectations, which can be done recursively.
Before proceeding further, let’s focus on two variables,
D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}
and
Dg_{i}^{(l)}
.
Their initialisation
D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}
and
Dh_{i}^{(L)}
are the same, and the recursive definition also matches, so they are equal always.
This implies that

\displaystyle Dg_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)},

\displaystyle Dg_{j}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=D_{\beta}J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)},

\displaystyle Dh_{i}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)},

\displaystyle Dh_{j}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}.

This implies that most of the expectations are equal, thereby we only need to compute three expectations, (
17
), (
18
), and (
22
).
All other expectations can be reduced to these three expectations, by equivalence we just shown or symmetry.

Before giving the proof of all equations, we first handle simple cases.
Equation (
17
) is a term that also appears in the standard NTK, and can be computed with the following recurrence relation,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2},

(26)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\times\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(27)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l-1)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l-1)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

(28)

where we used the independency of
Z
-variables
Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}
and
Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}
, this gives

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]=\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l+1)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l+1)}}\right],

and resolving recurrence relations shows

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]=\kappa^{2}\prod_{u=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}.

Now we can extend this approach to all the variables.
The first steps for (
26
) are

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2},

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2},

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(L)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(L)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}.

For the recursion step for the activation application corresponding to (
27
), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

(29)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}\cdot Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}+\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}\cdot\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

(30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}\cdot Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}+\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\cdot Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}+\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\cdot Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{J_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\ddot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)Z^{J_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{i}^{(l)}}\right)\dot{\phi}\left(Z^{g_{j}^{(l)}}\right)\right]\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha\beta}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{0\beta}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right].

(31)

Finally for the recursion step corresponding to matrix multiplication (
28
), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dh_{i}^{(l-1)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l-1)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{Dg_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l-1)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l-1)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l-1)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l-1)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right].

Our final task is solving the recurrence relations,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dg_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\prod_{u=l+1}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{Dh_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}\sum_{u_{\alpha}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha\beta}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}J_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{0\beta}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}J_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}h_{i}^{(l)}}Z^{D_{\beta}h_{j}^{(l)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha\beta}\prod_{u=l+1}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{2}\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\sum_{u_{\beta}=l+1}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\beta})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{0\beta}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{2}\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{0\beta}\sum_{u_{\alpha}=l+1}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}

\displaystyle\quad+\Gamma^{(l)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[Z^{D_{\alpha}g_{i}^{(l+1)}}Z^{D_{\beta}g_{j}^{(l+1)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\sum_{u^{\prime}=l}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u^{\prime})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha\beta}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq l^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{\alpha}=l\\
u_{\beta}=l\\
u_{\alpha}\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\Gamma^{(u_{\alpha})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{\alpha 0}\Gamma^{(u_{\beta})}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{0\beta}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=l\\
u\neq u_{\alpha}\\
u\neq u_{\beta}\end{subarray}}^{L-1}\Gamma^{(u)}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})_{00}.

Together with some trivial computations:

\displaystyle\left\langle h^{(0)}(x^{(i)}),h^{(0)}(x^{(j)})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial h^{(0)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}},h^{(0)}(x^{(j)})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle e_{\alpha-1},x^{(j)}\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle h^{(0)}(x^{(i)}),\frac{\partial h^{(0)}(x^{(j)})}{\partial x_{\beta-1}^{(j)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle x^{(i)},e_{\beta-1}\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial h^{(0)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}},\frac{\partial h^{(0)}(x^{(j)})}{\partial x_{\beta-1}^{(j)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle e_{\alpha-1},e_{\beta-1}\rangle,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(L+1)}(x)},\frac{\partial f(x)}{\partial g^{(L+1)}(x)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}

we can show the convergence of every inner product in the layer-wise description of the finite JNTK, which proves the desired convergence of the finite JNTK.

To utilise this theorem, we need a version that gives a convergence rate.
Since the Tensor Program framework does not give an explicit convergence rate, we will use an implicit function to give a convergence rate.

Remark H.1
.

There exists function
F_{2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
, so that if the width of network
d
is large enough,

d\geq F_{2}\left(x^{(1:N)},L,\delta,\epsilon\right),

then the finite JNTK and the limiting JNTK satisfy

\left\|\Theta_{d,\theta_{0}}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})-\kappa^{2}\Theta(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})\right\|_{F}\leq\kappa^{2}\epsilon

for all
i,j\in[N]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Appendix I
Finite JNTK After Perturbation

Theorem I.1
.

Let
\theta_{0}=\{W_{0}^{(l)}\}_{l=1}^{L+1}
be the weights of the
L
-layer neural network, initialised with
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Let
\omega
be small enough so that it satisfies

\omega\leq O\left((\log d)^{-2L}\right).

We also assume that the width is large enough to satisfy

d\geq\Omega(\exp(L)Nd_{0}/\delta).

Now let
\theta_{\mathcal{A}}=\{W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\}_{l=1}^{L+1}
be the ‘trained’ network, which is close to the initialisation:

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\omega\log d&\text{ if }l=1,\\
\omega\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{ if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\infty&\text{ if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{ if }l=1,\\
\omega\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{ if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega\log d&\text{ if }l=L+1.\end{cases}

Then with probability at least
1-\delta
over the randomness of
\theta_{0}
, for any choice of
\theta_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfying the assumption, the entries of the finite JNTK satisfy

\left\|\Theta_{d,\theta_{0}}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})-\Theta_{d,\theta_{\mathcal{A}}}(x^{(i)},x^{(j)})\right\|\leq\kappa^{2}\omega\exp(O(L))\log d.

for all
i,j\in[N]
.

In the statement of this Theorem, we introduced two weights
W_{0}^{(l)}
and
W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}
each representing the initialised weight at time
0
and the
A
dversarially perturbated weights.
Using these matrices defines two separate forward computations, which we write as

\displaystyle g_{0}^{(1)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{0}^{(1)}x,

\displaystyle g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}x,

\displaystyle h_{0}^{(1)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\phi\left(g_{0}^{(1)}(x)\right),

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\phi\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}(x)\right),

\displaystyle g_{0}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x),

\displaystyle g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x),

\displaystyle h_{0}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\phi\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right),

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\phi\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right),

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}W_{0}^{(L+1)}h_{0}^{(L)}(x),

\displaystyle f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{d_{L}}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L+1)}h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L)}(x).

We often omit function argument
x
when it is clear from the context.

Before stating the required lemmas and proofs, we first give the brief proof idea of Theoerm 9.
By the layer-wise decomposition of the finite JNTK in (
16
), it is enough to show that each layer-wise part of the finite JNTK is close to its initialisation.
To show this, we can show it by showing that the inner products appearing in each summand are close to their initialisation after perturbation, and at initialisation, the inner products are bounded with high probability.
Again, to show that the inner products after perturbation stay constant, we need to show at initialisation the norm of vectors are bounded with high probability, and their difference after perturbation is small.
Overall, we need to show the following informal lemmas, the activations and their norms are bounded with high probability:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{l-1}}}\left\|h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})\right\|,\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{l-1}}}\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|\leq\tilde{O}(1),

(32)

the backpropagated gradients of pre-activations are bounded with high probability:

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x^{(i)})}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}\right\|,\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}\right\|\leq\kappa\tilde{O}(1),

(33)

the activations stay close to their initialisation:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{l-1}}}\left\|h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})-h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})\right\|,\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{l-1}}}\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|\leq\omega\tilde{O}(1),

(34)

and the backpropagated gradients stay close to their initialisation:

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x^{(i)})}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}-\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}\right\|,\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}-\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}\right\|\leq\kappa\omega\tilde{O}(1).

(35)

Here we used
\tilde{O}(1)
to denote that there is implicit dependency over the logarithmic factor of
d
.

I.1
Bounds at initialisation

In this section, we will prove bounds holding at initialisation, especially the lemmas formalising Equation
32
and
33
.
All the lemmas here are stated for single data
x
, but we can apply the Union-bound to data indices, showing that the results hold for all
x^{(i)}
with probability at least
1-N\cdot\delta
.
Similarly for those applied to the Jacobian values, we can apply the Union-bound to the input dimension indices, showing equivalent results with probability at least
1-Nd_{0}\delta
.

Lemma I.2
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
If the network width satisfies both
d\geq\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
and
d\geq\exp(\Omega(L))
, then

\left\|g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|\leq 2\sqrt{d},\qquad\left\|h_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|\leq 2\sqrt{d},

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

We prove this lemma by induction on the conditioned activations.
Our inductive assumption is

\left|\frac{\|h_{0}^{(l)}\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|\leq\frac{G_{l}(\beta)}{2G_{L}(\beta)}\text{ with probability at least }1-\frac{l\cdot\delta}{L}

for some
\beta>0
that will be computed later.

To prove the induction step for this assumption, we will use the following claim:

Claim :

If
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}}
satisfy

\left|\|v\|^{2}-1\right|\leq\epsilon

with
\epsilon<1/2
, then for
W\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d^{\prime}}
and
W_{ij}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,

\left|\frac{\left\|\phi(Wv)\right\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|\leq\frac{1}{2G_{\beta}(L)}+\beta\epsilon

for some
\beta
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{L}
.

For the proof of claim, we first apply the triangle inequality to decompose the difference as

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\|\phi(Wv)\right\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\|\phi(Wv)\right\|^{2}}{d}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]\right|+\left|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]-1\right|

where we can rewrite the first term as concentration inequality,

\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi(\|v\|Z_{i})^{2}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]\right|.

The random variable here is sub-exponential, whose sub-exponential norm is bounded as

\displaystyle\|\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\|_{\mathrm{SE}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\phi(\|v\|Z)\|_{\mathrm{SG}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}^{2}\|\|v\|Z\|_{\mathrm{SG}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}^{2}\|v\|^{2}\|Z\|_{\mathrm{SG}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4M_{1}^{2}}{\ln 2}.

(36)

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
By Bernstein’s inequality (Corollary 2.8.3 of
(
Vershynin, 2018
)
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi(\|v\|Z_{i})^{2}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]\right|\leq\frac{1}{2G_{\beta}(L)}

with a probability of at least

1-2\exp\left(-c\max\left(\frac{1}{4K^{2}G_{\beta}(L)^{2}},\frac{1}{2KG_{\beta}(L)}\right)\cdot d\right)

where
K
is the sub-exponential norm computed in (
36
).

For the second term, we can observe that this is similar to local Lipschitz-ness, which is clearer if we rewrite it as

\left|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]-1\right|=\left|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(1\cdot Z)^{2}\right]\right|.

Which can be bounded as

\displaystyle\left|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}\right]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(Z)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}-\phi(Z)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left|\phi(\|v\|Z)^{2}-\phi(Z)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left|\phi(\|v\|Z)-\phi(Z)\right|\left|\phi(\|v\|Z)+\phi(Z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}|\|v\|-1|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left|\phi(\|v\|Z)+\phi(Z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}|\|v\|-1|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left|\phi(0)+(\phi(\|v\|Z)-\phi(0))+\phi(0)+(\phi(Z)-\phi(0))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}|\|v\|-1|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\left|2\phi(0)+M_{1}(\|v\|+1)Z\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{1}|\|v\|-1|\left(2\phi(0)+M_{1}(\|v\|+1)\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}\left(2\phi(0)+M_{1}\frac{5}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)|\|v\|^{2}-1|

where we used the assumption that
\frac{1}{2}\leq\|v\|\leq\frac{3}{2}
in the last inequality.
Combining these two inequalities, we prove the claim

\left|\frac{\|\phi(Wv)\|^{2}}{d}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2G_{\beta}(L)}+\beta\epsilon

with
\beta=M_{1}\left(2\phi(0)+M_{1}\frac{5}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)
.

At layer one, we can show that

\left|\frac{\|h_{0}^{(1)}\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|\leq\frac{1}{2G_{\beta}(L)}

with probability at least
1-\delta/L
, which proves the inductive assumption’s base case
l=1
.

The inductive step is similar, inductive assumption implies the condition of the claim, which then gives

\left|\frac{\|h_{0}^{(l)}\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|\leq\frac{1}{2G_{\beta}(L)}+\beta\left|\frac{\|h_{0}^{(l-1)}\|^{2}}{d}-1\right|=\frac{1+\beta G_{\beta}(l-1)}{2G_{\beta}(L)}=\frac{G_{\beta}(l)}{2G_{\beta}(L)}

with probability at least
1-l\delta/L
by Union-bound on the previous layer’s inductive assumption and the claim’s event.
∎

Lemma I.3
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
If the network width satisfies
d\geq\Omega(\log(L/\delta))
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq C^{l}M_{1}^{l-1}\sqrt{d},\qquad\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq(CM_{1})^{l}\sqrt{d},

holds for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Let’s recall the recursive definition of Jacobian first:

\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}=\dot{\phi}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l-1)}h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l-1)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right).

Since
\phi
is
M_{1}
-Lipschitz, we can bound as

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|.

To bound the operator norm of the random matrix, we use the standard result (Theorem 4.4.5 of
(
Vershynin, 2018
)
) which shows that

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|\leq C\left(2+\frac{t}{\sqrt{d}}\right)

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t^{2})
.
Plugging in
t=\sqrt{d}
, we need to set
1-2\exp(-d)\geq 1-\delta/L
, which is achieved by our assumption on
d
.

We have

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq 3CM_{1}\sqrt{d}

with probability at least
1-\delta/L
in a similar way as the recursive step.
Then applying the recursive steps proves the result.
∎

We can derive similar bounds for the backpropagated gradients.

Lemma I.4
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
If the network width satisfy
d\geq\Omega(\log(L/\delta))
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa(CM_{1})^{L+1-l},\qquad\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa C^{L-l}M_{1}^{L+1-l},

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

The proof idea of this lemma is similar to the proof of Lemma
I.3
.
Here we can recursively bound the backpropagated gradients,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\dot{\phi}\left(W_{0}^{(l-1)}h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right\|\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|.

Similar to the Lemma
I.3
, we obtain

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right\|\leq 3C

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-d)\geq 1-\delta/L
.

For the initial case, we have

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(L)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa 3M_{1}C

with probability at least
1-\delta/L
, and applying the recursive steps proves our result.
∎

Lemma I.5
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
If the network width satisfy
d\geq\Omega(\log(L/\delta))
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa M_{2}C^{\prime}(CM_{1})^{L-1}\sum_{i=0}^{L-l}(CM_{1})^{i},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa M_{2}C^{\prime}C^{l}M_{1}^{L-1}\sum_{i=0}^{L-l-1}(CM_{1})^{i},

holds for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Let’s first unfold the definition,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle=\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\odot\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}\right)\odot\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle=\ddot{\phi}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)

\displaystyle+\dot{\phi}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)

To bound this vector’s norm, we will analyse each term,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle+M_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|.

For the first term, we use Lemma
I.3
and
I.4
, which allow us to argue that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq(CM_{1})^{l-1}\sqrt{d},\qquad\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa M_{2}C^{\prime}(CM_{1})^{L-1}\sum_{i=0}^{L-l}(CM_{1})^{i}

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
.
Recall that these two lemmas assume the conditioning on the event

\left\|W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{d}

for all
l=1,\ldots,L+1
.
We can implement this conditioning via multiplying additional random variable
\alpha_{l}\leq 1
, whose existence is guaranteed by stochastic dominance of
\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|
over conditioned random variable
\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}
.

\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\bigg|\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{dist.}}}{{=}}\frac{\alpha_{l}}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\left(\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\bigg|\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\right)

where
X|E
is the conditional distribution of
X
on the event
E
, and
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}
means that these two random vectors are distributionally equal.
The formal proof of this argument is given in
I.6
.

Now to bound the first term, we can proceed as

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|\bigg|\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\left(\frac{\alpha_{l}}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\left(\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\bigg|\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\odot\left(\frac{\alpha_{l+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\left(\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\right)\right)\Bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\kappa(CM_{1})^{L-1}\sqrt{d}\left\|\left(\frac{\alpha_{l}}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}v\right)\odot\left(\frac{\alpha_{l+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}w\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\kappa(CM_{1})^{L-1}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l)}v\right)\odot\left(\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}w\right)\right\|

where
v
and
w
follows uniform distribution over
S^{d-1}
.

So to bound this vector’s norm, we should bound the following quantity,

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}^{2}W_{i}^{2}

where
Z_{i},W_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
This distribution is known to be sub-Weibull with parameter
\theta=1/2
,
(
Zhang and Wei, 2022
)
with the concentration bound (Proposition 3 of
(
Zhang and Wei, 2022
)
)

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}^{2}W_{i}^{2}\leq C_{1,1/2}+C_{2,1/2}

for some absolute constants
C_{1,1/2},C_{2,1/2}>0
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-d)
.

In summary, we obtain

\left\|\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|\leq\kappa C^{\prime}(CM_{1})^{L-1}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-d)/(2L)
.

The second term can be bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|\dot{\phi}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}\right)\odot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|\bigg|\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right)\right\|\bigg|\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}C\left\|\left(\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\bigg|\forall l.\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\right)\right\|.

Summing up, including the fact that

\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(L)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa C

with probability at least
1-\exp(-d)/(2L)
, we result

\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa M_{2}C^{\prime}(CM_{1})^{L-1}\sum_{i=0}^{L-l}(CM_{1})^{i}.

∎

Lemma I.6
.

Suppose some random variable
X
with
X>0
almost surely.
For any
M>0
, there exists some random variable
Z\leq 1
that satisfies

Z\cdot X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}X|X\leq M.

Proof.

We prove this via stochastic dominance.

We can show that
X
(first order) stochastically dominates
X|X\leq M
, by showing that for any non-decreasing
u
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)]

\displaystyle=P(X>M)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X>M]+P(X\leq M)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X\leq M]

\displaystyle\geq P(X>M)u(M)+P(X\leq M)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X\leq M]

\displaystyle\geq P(X>M)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X\leq M]+P(X\leq M)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X\leq M]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}[u(X)|X\leq M].

Now from the property of stochastic dominance, this implies that there exists some random variable
W\geq 0
such that

X=(X|X\leq M)+W

which then after rearranging, we obtain

X|X\leq M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{X-W}{X}\cdot X

with
Z=(X-W)/X\leq 1
which is guaranteed by
X>0
and
W\geq 0
.
∎

We also include a corollary that proves similar results for
\infty
-norms.
These results are in general not required for standard NTK analysis, but for JNTK, we need to have a tight bound of element-wise product
\|v\odot w\|
. Without such a bound, we end up with an additional
\sqrt{d}
factor.

Corollary I.7
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
If the network depth satisfy both
d\geq\Omega(1/\delta)
and
d\geq\exp(\Omega(L))
, then

\displaystyle\left\|g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\log d}

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq O(\exp(L)\sqrt{\log d}),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}}O(\exp(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}}\exp(O(L)),

holds for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

Note that all these three random vectors are generated by matrix multiplication with Gaussian matrix,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l)}\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial ho{l}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}.

This allows us to argue that each coordinate of the vectors we are curious about is conditionally Gaussian, where their variance is given by the norm of the vectors in RHS.

As we’ve done in the proof of Lemma
I.5
, the boundedness of the vector norms requires the conditioning of weight matrices, to
\|W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}
for some
C>0
, and applying Lemma
I.6
similarly show that this conditioning makes the norm smaller, so we can safely ignore them.

So, all three problems collapse to the following probabilistic bound,

P\left(\max_{i}|Z_{i}|\leq\sqrt{\log d}+t\right)\geq 1-2\exp(-t^{2})

where
Z_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Plugging
t=\sqrt{\log d}
, we obtain probability bound
1-\frac{2}{d}
.
∎

Before ending this section, we will give intuitive big-O notation-based results, which say that if the network width satisfy

d\geq\Omega(\log(Ld_{0}/\delta))

then

\displaystyle\left\|g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|h_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{d},

(37)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\exp(L))\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\exp(L))\sqrt{d},

(38)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa O(\exp(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa O(\exp(L)),

(39)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L)),

(40)

for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
simultaneously, with probability at least
1-\delta
.

I.2
Bounds after perturbation

We now prove that the vectors that appeared in previous sections are stable after perturbation of the weight matrices.
We consider
\sqrt{d}\omega
perturbation over the network weights, which models the perturbation due to the gradient.
We additionally assume the
\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
perturbation over the
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}
and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{1}
, which are the maximum of column sum and row sums, respectively.

We will later let the perturbation
\omega
be small enough to prove the global convergence, so throughout this section, we will assume that
\omega\leq 1
to write
\omega^{p}\leq\omega
.

Since it is hard to interpret the order of quantifiers in the lemmas, we will mention here explicitly, which applies to all lemmas in Section
I.2
.
We first specify the fail probability and network depth.
Then there exists a bound on
\omega
and
d
, which implies that the results hold for all choices of
W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}
satisfying the condition on
\omega
, for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
simultaneously, with probability at least
1-\delta
.
This result holds for any choice of
x
, so to extend this proof to all training data
x^{(1:N)}
, we can apply the Union-bound to results weakened fail probability guarantee
1-N\delta
.

Lemma I.8
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
.
If
\omega\leq 1
, and the network width satisfies both
d\geq\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
and
d\geq\exp(\Omega(L))
, then

\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-h_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq 2\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}((C+1)M_{1})^{i}\omega\sqrt{d}=O(\exp(L)\omega\sqrt{d}).

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

This is proven by recursive stability analysis, at layer
l=1
,

\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}-g_{0}^{(1)}\right\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\|x\|=\omega\sqrt{d},

and for the latter layers,

\displaystyle\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-h_{0}^{(l)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-g_{0}^{(l)}\right\|,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-g_{0}^{(l)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}h_{0}^{(l-1)}-g_{0}^{(l)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right\|\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}-h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|+\omega\|h_{0}^{(l-1)}\|

\displaystyle\leq(C+\omega)\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}-h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|+2\omega\sqrt{d}

where we applied Lemma
I.2
which holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, and norm bound on the
W_{0}^{(l)}
as we’ve done in the proof of Lemma
I.4
.

Summing up, we results

\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-h_{0}^{(l)}\right\|\leq((C+1)M_{1})^{l-1}\omega\sqrt{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{l-1}2M_{1}^{l-i}(C+1)^{l-i-1}\omega\sqrt{d}

∎

We also prove a similar version for the
L_{\infty}
-norm.

Lemma I.9
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
, and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
for all
2\leq l\leq L
and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}-W_{0}^{(1)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\log d
.
If
\omega\leq 1
, and the network width satisfies
d\geq L/\delta
, then

\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-h_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{L}.

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

This has a similar recursive analysis, as

\displaystyle\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}-g_{0}^{(1)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\omega\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-h_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-g_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-g_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}-h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\log d\left\|h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}-h_{0}^{(l-1)}\right\|_{\infty}+C\omega\log d.

∎

Lemma I.10
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
.
If
\omega\leq 1
, and the network width satisfy both
d\geq\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
and
d\geq\exp(\Omega(L))
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq((C+1)M_{1})^{l-1}\omega\sqrt{d\log d}+\sum_{i=1}^{l-1}(1+C)^{l-i-1}M_{1}^{l-i}(CM_{1})^{i-1}\omega\sqrt{d}

holds for all
l\in[L+1]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

By using Lemma
I.3
and probabilistic bounds on
W_{0}^{(l)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\|e_{\alpha-1}\|=\omega\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)\odot\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)-\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right)\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\right\|+M_{2}\left\|\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)-g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\right\|+M_{2}\left\|\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)-g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right\|O(\exp(L)\sqrt{\log d})

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\right\|+M_{2}O\left(\exp(L)\sqrt{d\log d}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq(C+\omega)\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|+(CM_{1})^{l-1}\omega\sqrt{d}.

Here, we applied Lemma
I.7
to handle the bound for the Hadamard product, which requires
d\geq\Omega(L/\delta)
assumption.

Summing up, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|\leq\exp(O(L))\omega\sqrt{d\log d}.

∎

Lemma I.11
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l
,
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
for all
2\leq l\leq L
and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}-W_{0}^{(1)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\log d
.
If
\omega\leq 1
, and the network width satisfies
d\geq L/\delta
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{2L}

holds for all
l
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

We can prove this by simple analysis,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\omega\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)\odot\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\left(\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)-\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right)\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}+M_{2}\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)-g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}+M_{2}\omega\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{L}\sqrt{\log d}

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\log d\left\|\frac{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial h_{0}^{(l-1)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right\|_{\infty}+\omega(\log d)^{3/2}\exp(O(L))

∎

Lemma I.12
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
.
If
\omega\leq 1
, and the network width satisfies both
d\geq\Omega(1/\delta)
and
d\geq\exp(L)
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}}\right\|\leq\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))O(\sqrt{\log d})

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

We prove this by a similar approach to Lemma
I.10
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(L)}(x)}\right\|\leq\kappa\omega,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\odot\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)-\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right)\odot\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|+M_{2}\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)-g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{d}}O\left(\exp(L)\sqrt{\log d}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|+\kappa M_{2}\omega O\left(\exp(L)\sqrt{\log d}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq 3C\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}}(x)\right\|.

∎

Lemma I.13
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
,
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{1}\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
for
2\leq l\leq L
, and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L+1)}-W_{0}^{(L+1)}\|_{1}\leq\omega\log d
.
If
\omega\leq 1
and the network width satisfies
d\geq\Omega(L/\delta)
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}}

holds for all
l\in[L]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(L)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa\omega\frac{\log d}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\odot\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\left(\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)\right)-\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right)\right)\odot\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\right\|_{\infty}+M_{2}\left\|g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)-g_{0}^{(l)}(x)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq M_{1}\left\|\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\right\|_{\infty}+M_{2}\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{L}\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\left(W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}-W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\kappa\omega\log d\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}}\exp(O(L))+C\log d\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right\|_{\infty}

∎

Lemma I.14
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
,
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
for all
2\leq l\leq L
, and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(1)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{\infty}\leq\omega\log d
.
If
\omega\leq(\log d)^{-2L}
and the network width satisfies both
d\geq\Omega(1/\delta)
and
d\geq\exp(\Omega(L))
, then

\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}}\right\|\leq\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))\log d

holds for all
l\in[L]
and
\alpha\in[d_{0}]
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

We first unroll the definitions,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L)}(x)}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(L)}(x)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{0},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}-W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(W_{0}^{(l+1)}\right)^{\intercal}\left(\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l+1)}(x)}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l+1)}(x)}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\odot\ddot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)

(41)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{A}}(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}-\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\right)\odot\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\ddot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)

(42)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\odot\left(\frac{\partial g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}-\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\right)\odot\ddot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)

(43)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial f_{0}(x)}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}(x)}\odot\frac{\partial g_{0}^{(l)}(x)}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}}\odot\left(\ddot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)-\ddot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}\right)\right)

(44)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}\right)\odot\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)

(45)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}\odot\left(\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}\right)-\dot{\sigma}\left(g_{0}^{(l)}\right)\right).

(46)

Then we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn28}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn29}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\kappa\omega^{2}\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{2L+1/2},

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn30}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\kappa\omega\exp(O(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn31}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\kappa\omega O(\exp(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn32}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{\mathcal{A}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}}-\frac{\partial J(f_{0})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial h_{0}^{(l)}}\right\|,

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{eqn:eqn33}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\kappa\omega O(\exp(L))\sqrt{\log d}.

Summing up the results gives our final result.
∎

Lemma I.15
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and the network depth
L
.
Suppose that
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|\leq\omega\sqrt{d}
for all
l\in[L+1]
,
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(l)}-W_{0}^{(l)}\|_{1}\leq\omega\sqrt{d}\log d
for
2\leq l\leq L
, and
\|W_{\mathcal{A}}^{(L+1)}-W_{0}^{(L+1)}\|_{1}\leq\omega\log d
.
If
\omega\leq(\log d)^{-L}
and the network width satisfies
d\geq\Omega(L/\delta)
