\displaystyle u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k}-\eta\nabla_{\theta_{k}}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k}))-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\eta}\left\langle\nabla_{\theta_{k}}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k}),\frac{\partial u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k,s})}{\partial\theta_{k,s}}\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{\eta}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})^{\intercal}\Lambda^{\oplus N}\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\Lambda^{\oplus N}ds

where we introduced two extensions of notations:

\theta_{k,s}=\theta_{k}-s\nabla_{\theta_{k}}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})

and

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x,x^{\prime})_{00}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x)}{\partial\theta_{k}},\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k,s}}(x^{\prime})}{\partial\theta_{k,s}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha 0}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\theta_{k}},\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k,s}}(x^{\prime})}{\partial\theta_{k,s}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x,x^{\prime})_{0\beta}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x)}{\partial\theta_{k}},\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k,s}})(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial\theta_{k,s}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x,x^{\prime})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x)_{\alpha-1}}{\partial\theta_{k}},\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k,s}})(x^{\prime})_{\beta-1}}{\partial\theta_{k,s}}\right\rangle

so that
\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})
is defined in the same manner of
\Theta_{d,\theta}
.

If we can show that this empirical JNTK also satisfies the following for all
k,s\in[0,\eta]
:

\lambda_{\min}(\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)}))\geq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\lambda_{0}

we can prove that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{y}-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k}),u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{\eta}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})^{\intercal}\Lambda^{\oplus N}\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\Lambda^{\oplus N}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{\eta}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})^{\intercal}\lambda_{\min}(\Lambda^{\oplus N}\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\Lambda^{\oplus N})(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{2N}\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}.

So if we can show that the third term,
\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\|_{2}^{2}
is small enough so that it can’t cancel out the second term, we can show that the loss decreases every step by multiplicative factor
(1-\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}/2)
.
Our proof is done by the induction on two properties, (1) the weights stay close to their initialisation, and (2) the term
\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\|^{2}
has roughly order
\eta^{2}\kappa^{4}
, which then shows the linear convergence.

We first introduce the
Q(k)
indexed by
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, which is defined as

\displaystyle Q(k)=\frac{1}{2}(d_{0}+1)\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N}\right)^{i/2}

which will upper bound
\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}(\theta_{i})}
.
Note that this quantity itself is again bounded globally, by

\displaystyle Q(k)

\displaystyle\leq Q(\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{0}+1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N}\right)^{i/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{0}+1}{2(1-\sqrt{1-\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}/4N})}.

We then define
\omega(k)
as

\omega(k)=\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}}Q(k)\frac{(\log d)^{3L}}{\sqrt{d}}

which similarly bounded by

\omega(k)\leq\omega(\infty)=\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}}Q(\infty)\frac{(\log d)^{3L}}{\sqrt{d}}.

Then we further bound this quantity by
\omega
which will be used as in the gradient flow case,

\displaystyle\omega(\infty)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}^{3}}\frac{(\log d)^{3L}}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{\eta}{1-\sqrt{1-\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}/4N}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}^{3}}\frac{(\log d)^{3L}}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{8N}{\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\exp(O(L))(Nd_{0})^{3/2}(\log d)^{3L}}{\kappa\lambda\lambda_{0}\sqrt{d}}}_{\omega\coloneqq}.

where the inequality holds since
\eta/(1-\sqrt{1-t\eta})
monotonically decreases at
\eta\geq 0
, with its maximum
2/t
attained at
\eta=0
.

We first state the lemma that will be used in both induction hypotheses.

Lemma L.1
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and network depth
L
.
Suppose that the network width
d
and
\omega(\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(Nd_{0}\exp(L)/\delta),

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\omega^{-2}),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{N\exp(\Omega(L))(\log d)^{3L}}\right),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq(\log d)^{-2L}.

With probability at least
1-\delta
over the random initialisation, the following holds for all
k_{0}
and any
l
.
If for all
0\leq k<k_{0}
, all the weights stay close to their initialisation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

then the weight update is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(L+1)}(k+1)-W^{(L+1)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}(\log d)^{3L+1},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}(\log d)^{2L},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(L+1)}(k+1)-W^{(L+1)}(k)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}}

for
2\leq l\leq L
.

Proof.

We can unfold the weight update rule to get

\displaystyle W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left((f_{\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})-y^{(i)})\frac{\partial f_{\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d_{0}}J(f_{\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}\frac{\partial J(f_{\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right)

which then shows

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\left|f_{\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})-y^{(i)}\right|+\sqrt{\lambda}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{d_{0}}\left|J(f_{\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}\right|\right)\max_{i\in[N],\alpha\in[d_{0}]}\left\{\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{p},\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\sqrt{(d_{0}+1)}\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|}{\sqrt{N}}\max_{i\in[N],\alpha\in[d_{0}]}\left\{\left\|\frac{\partial f_{\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{p},\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{p}\right\}

where we used assumption that
\lambda\leq 1
.
The matrix Jacobians can be further bounded as in the proof of Lemma
J.2
: for
2\leq l\leq L
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(1)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa O(\exp(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(1)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa O(\exp(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(L+1)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa O(\exp(L)),

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(L+1)}(k)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(1)}(k)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(1)}(k)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\frac{(\log d)^{3L+1}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{L},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{3L+1},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{2L},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(l)}(k)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))(\log d)^{2L},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})}{\partial W^{(L+1)}(k)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\frac{(\log d)^{L}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}}{\partial W^{(L+1)}(k)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\exp(O(L))\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}}.

We obtain the following inequalities for
2\leq l\leq L
:

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(L+1)}(k+1)-W^{(L+1)}(k)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\log d,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}(\log d)^{3L+1},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k+1)-W^{(l)}(k)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}(\log d)^{2L},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(L+1)}(k+1)-W^{(L+1)}(k)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}\frac{(\log d)^{2L}}{\sqrt{d}}.

∎

Lemma L.2
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and network depth
L
.
Suppose that the network width
d
and
\omega(\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(Nd_{0}\exp(L)/\delta),

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\omega^{-2}),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{N\exp(\Omega(L))(\log d)^{3L}}\right),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq(\log d)^{-2L}.

Also assume that the
\kappa
is small, and network is intialised with
\mathcal{L}(\theta_{0})\leq d_{0}+1
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
over the random initialisation, the following holds for all
k_{0}
and any
l
.
If for all
0\leq k<k_{0}
, all the weights stay close to their initialisation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

and linear convergence, i.e.,

\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})\leq\mathcal{L}(\theta_{0})\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4}\right)^{k_{0}}

holds, then

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k_{0})-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k_{0})\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k_{0})-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k_{0})\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k_{0})\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k_{0})-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k_{0})\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k_{0})\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

holds.

Proof.

Combining the linear convergence assumption with the assumption on
\mathcal{L}(\theta_{0})
, we can see that the following holds for all
0\leq k<k_{0}
,

\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})\leq\mathcal{L}(\theta_{0})\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4M}\right)^{k}.

Then the result is obtained via triangle inequality with Lemma
L.1
.
∎

Lemma L.3
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and network depth
L
.
Suppose that the network width
d
and
\omega(\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(Nd_{0}\exp(L)/\delta),

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\omega^{-2}),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{N\exp(\Omega(L))(\log d)^{3L}}\right),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq(\log d)^{-2L}.

With probability at least
1-\delta
over the random initialisation, if all the weights stay close to their initialisation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

then

\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\right\|^{2}\leq\eta^{2}\kappa^{4}\exp(O(L))Nd_{0}^{2}\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}.

Proof.

Most of the technical computations are done in the proof of Lemma
L.1
, and the remaining job is using those bounds on matrix differences to bound the output differences.
The followings hold for
1\leq l\leq L
:

\displaystyle\left\|g_{k+1}^{(1)}(x^{(i)})-g_{k}^{(1)}(x^{(i)})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|\left\|x^{(i)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})},

\displaystyle\left\|h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})-h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}\left\|g_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})-g_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|,

\displaystyle\left\|g_{k+1}^{(l+1)}(x^{(i)})-g_{k}^{(l+1)}(x^{(i)})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W^{(l+1)}(k+1)-W^{(l+1)}(k)\right\|\left\|h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W^{(l+1)}(k)\right\|\left\|h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})-h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}+\exp(O(L))\left\|h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})-h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|,

\displaystyle\left|f_{d,\theta_{k+1}}(x^{(i)})-f_{d,\theta_{k}}(x^{(i)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\kappa\left|g_{k+1}^{(L+1)}(x^{(i)})-g_{k}^{(L+1)}(x^{(i)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa^{2}\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}.

Similarly, the following hold for
1\leq l\leq L
:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial g_{k}^{(1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(1)}(k+1)-W^{(1)}(k)\right\|\left\|e_{\alpha-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})},

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{2}\left\|g_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})-g_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})\right\|\left\|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|_{\infty}+M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial g_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}+M_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial g_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(l+1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial g_{k}^{(l+1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W^{(l+1)}(k+1)-W^{(l+1)}(k)\right\|\left\|\frac{\partial h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|W^{(l+1)}(k)\right\|\left\|\frac{\partial h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}+\exp(O(L))\left\|\frac{\partial h_{k+1}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial h_{k}^{(l)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right\|,

\displaystyle\left|J(f_{d,\theta_{k+1}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}-J(f_{d,\theta_{k}})(x^{(i)})_{\alpha-1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\kappa\left|\frac{\partial g_{k+1}^{(L+1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}-\frac{\partial g_{k}^{(L+1)}(x^{(i)})}{\partial x_{\alpha-1}^{(i)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\kappa^{2}\exp(O(L))\sqrt{Nd_{0}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})}.

Combining these, we obtain

\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\right\|^{2}\leq\eta^{2}\kappa^{4}\exp(O(L))Nd_{0}^{2}\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}.

∎

Lemma L.4
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and network depth
L
.
Suppose that the network width
d
, learning rate
\eta
, and
\omega(\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(Nd_{0}\exp(L)/\delta),

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\omega^{-2}),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{N\exp(\Omega(L))(\log d)^{3L}}\right),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq(\log d)^{-2L},

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N^{2}d_{0}^{2}}\exp(-\Omega(L)).

With probability at least
1-\delta
over the random initialisation, if all the weights stay close to their initialisation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

then

\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k+1})\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4}\right)\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k}).

Proof.

From the Lemma
L.2
, we can see that
W^{(l)}(k+1)
also satisfies similar bound, but with
\omega(k+1)
.
Using the convexity of matrix norms, we can see that

\displaystyle\left\|W_{k,s}^{(l)}-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k+1)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W_{k,s}^{(l)}-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k+1)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k+1)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W_{k,s}^{(l)}-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k+1)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k+1)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

for all
s\in[0,\eta]
, since
W_{k,s}^{(l)}
linearly interpolates
W^{(l)}(k)
and
W^{(l)}(k+1)
.

Using Theorem
I.1
, we can show that

\lambda_{\min}\left(\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\right)\geq\frac{\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{2}

which shows that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{y}-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k}),u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{s}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})^{\intercal}\Lambda^{\oplus N}\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\Lambda^{\oplus N}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{s}(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})^{\intercal}\lambda_{\min}\left(\Lambda^{\oplus N}\Theta_{d,\theta_{k},\theta_{k,s}}(x^{(1:N)},x^{(1:N)})\Lambda^{\oplus N}\right)(u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y})ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{2N}\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\right\|^{2}.

Also from Lemma
L.3
, we have

\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\right\|^{2}\leq\eta^{2}\kappa^{4}\exp(O(L))Nd_{0}^{2}\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}.

From the assumption on
\eta
, this term can be further bounded by

\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N}\left\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\right\|^{2}.

Combining these two inequalities, we obtain

\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k+1})-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N}\right)\|u_{\mathrm{nn}}(\theta_{k})-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}.

∎

Now we have all the ingredients to prove Theorem
4.5
.

Theorem L.5
.

Fix the fail probability
\delta>0
and network depth
L
.
Suppose that the network width
d
, learning rate
\eta
, and
\omega(\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(Nd_{0}\exp(L)/\delta),

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\omega^{-2}),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}\lambda}{N\exp(\Omega(L))(\log d)^{3L}}\right),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle\leq(\log d)^{-2L},

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N^{2}d_{0}^{2}}\exp(-\Omega(L)).

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
over the random initialisation, the following holds for all
k\geq 0
:

•

The weights stay close to their initialisation:

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\omega(k)\sqrt{d},

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=L+1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\|W^{(l)}(k)-W^{(l)}(0)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if }l=1,\\
\omega(k)\sqrt{d}\log d&\text{if }2\leq l\leq L,\\
\omega(k)\log d&\text{if }l=L+1,\end{cases}

•

Linear convergence happens:

\mathcal{L}(\theta_{k})\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}\lambda\lambda_{0}}{4N}\right)^{k}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{0}).

Proof.

The proof is immediate with induction on
k
, and the application of Lemma
L.4
and Lemma
L.2
.
∎
